## 2026-08-13 Scaffold fading `/validate-idea` — 1 confirmed unlock at the representation layer

| Components | Pattern | Unlock | Failure mode | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) §CPA (concreteness fading — the scaffold must be *withdrawn*, Fyfe et al. 2014) × [once-seen-never-forget-protocol](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) §C.4 (the per-concept image is permanent load-bearing infrastructure — "if the file dies, the encoding dies") | State ([software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §State — a visual moves `active → faded → retired`) constrained by **ISP** (nobody is forced to carry more rule than the visual needs) — **status: confirmed**, both rules now shipped | **The addressing/scaffold visual type split.** The two components read as a flat contradiction — one says *never remove the picture*, the other says *removing the picture is the point* — and the contradiction dissolves once you notice they govern **different objects**. A **Type A addressing visual** *is* the retrieval handle (fading it destroys the address); a **Type B scaffold visual** is a medium you reason *inside* (keeping it forever is the ceiling). Neither page had this distinction alone, and [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 1 had been governing both undifferentiated since 2026-05-07. Three things fall out: the two tests are **orthogonal, not competing** (Rule 1 tests picture-*sufficiency* — hide the text; Rule 9 tests picture-*independence* — hide the picture); [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) gains **Rule 9**, which turns out to be the **expertise-reversal effect** (Kalyuga/Sweller 2003+, already registered in the [glossary](./glossary.md) and owned by [drill-generator](./drill-generator.md)) applied to the representation layer for the first time — it was the one CLT effect on that page's own table with no rule implementing it; and [once-seen-never-forget-protocol](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) Stage R gains the covered-diagram probe it structurally lacked, since its axis 1 only ever asked *can you still see the image?* Validated 2026-08-13 via `/validate-idea` (keep-with-modification: scope to Type B, declaration-not-apparatus, route the checksum into Stage R rather than a new review surface). | Applying Rule 9 to Type A visuals — destroys the retrieval address and re-breaks OSNF; this is the ISP violation the unmodified proposal carried. Typing a visual "A" because fading it is inconvenient (the stricter obligation wins when a visual is genuinely both). Scaffold dependence going undiagnosed because the probe is never run — mitigated by the ≥3-cycle re-encode floor on `repr.scaffold_independent`. Rule 9 becoming decoration: the **dead-letter clause** (zero Type B visuals declared within 4 weeks → drop the rule) is what keeps it from being the ceremony [software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §The Main Constraint rejects. | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 1 §Two visual types + Rule 9, [once-seen-never-forget-protocol](./once-seen-never-forget-protocol.md) §Stage R, [singapore-math](./singapore-math.md) |

## 2026-08-20 Count-shape + concept glyph `/validate-idea` — 1 confirmed unlock at the representation layer

| Components | Pattern | Unlock | Failure mode | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Subitizing (the ~4-item pre-attentive count limit — Kaufman, Lord, Reese & Volkmann 1949; Trick & Pylyshyn 1994) × [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 2 (the *glyph size* — named as the smallest of four densities since 2026-05-07 but never given an address) × [UMTF](./universal-mental-tagging-framework.md) §Orthogonality Rules ("do not let the same cue mean two different things"; "keep mappings stable after assignment") | **Flyweight** ([software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §Flyweight — one shared symbol vocabulary reused across many concrete pages) over a **derived registry** (DRY + §Registries shard on their own retrieval axis: authoritative copy in page frontmatter, glyph→page view generated) — **status: confirmed**, both rules shipped with machine checks | **A page's structure becomes checkable without reading it.** Two independent moves, one payoff. (1) **Count-shape**: seating a 2–7 set on the polygon of its own size turns cardinality into a pre-attentive read, and — the load-bearing part — makes the set's *completeness* visible, because an empty corner shows before a single label does. A bulleted list cannot show you the bullet that isn't there. (2) **Concept glyph**: `glyph:` frontmatter gives Rule 2's long-stated glyph size an actual address, so a page becomes a manipulable token rather than a title you have to read. Uniqueness is what makes it an address rather than decoration, and it is exactly UMTF's sensory-tag discipline promoted from the mental encoding to the page layer — the wiki had been running the doctrine internally and never applied it to itself. The composition also settled an ownership question the wiki had left open: the four-part reading *name · rules · relations · distinguisher* is **NEDF's slots plus a category error** — relations are edges, so they leave the box; NEDF's **F** keeps the fourth corner, and Rule 7's boundary set comes free. | Drawing the polygon as a *node* (collides with Rule 4's shape reservations — circle/square/diamond are node types; the count-shape is an *arrangement*). Reaching for a nonagon instead of noticing the "set" is a sequence or two nested sets. Picking a glyph because it is pretty — the same decoration failure [feedback-visual-per-concept](./feedback-visual-per-concept.md) names for images, and the "if everything glows, nothing glows" failure UMTF names for priority tags. Minting a rival four-slot concept schema beside [NEDF](./nedf-overview.md) — the modification that `/validate-idea` imposed exists to prevent exactly that. Both rules carry a **dead-letter clause** due **2026-09-17** (5 declared count-shapes; 20 pages carrying `glyph:`) so neither becomes the ceremony [software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §The Main Constraint rejects. | [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rules 10 + 11, `tools/glyph_registry.py`, `wiki/_meta/glyph-registry.md` |

## 2026-08-22 The displacement ladder `/validate-idea` — 1 candidate unlock at the resource-stewardship ↔ tool-building layer

| Components | Pattern | Unlock | Failure mode | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| tools-over-intelligence §The principle (*"once cognition is frozen into an artifact, intelligence stops being the rate-limiting factor"*) × idle-resource-is-adversarial §The rate axis (rate-endogenous slope; the rate-converting property) × [software-design-principles-for-neural-os](./software-design-principles-for-neural-os.md) §The Test-Pyramid Convergence (*"you cannot steer position, only slope"*) | Strategy over an ordered ladder, rendered per [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 10 as a **ladder, not a polygon** (the members are ordered by displacement, so a polygon would assert a symmetry that isn't there) — **status: candidate**, promotion needs a second domain instance outside cognition | **The displacement ladder: raw capacity → endogenous rate → fossilized tool.** The argument tools-over-intelligence uses to dethrone raw intelligence runs again one level up, against the growth rate itself: a fossilized tool raises the slope for everyone who picks it up with *nobody's* rate changing. So each rung retires the one below as the binding constraint, and "which variable matters most" has an altitude-dependent answer rather than a single winner. Two things fall out. First, the recurring claim *"X is the master variable"* has a standard refutation shape in this wiki — find the artifact that makes X non-binding — and both prior instances (IQ, then rate) resolve the same way. Second, it supplies the *reason* §The converter ladder (skill → tool → system) is the operational move and a bare high slope is not: the rate is what you **convert into** rungs that outlive you, which is idle-resource-is-adversarial's rate-converting property read as an instruction instead of a description. Validated 2026-08-22 via `/validate-idea` (keep-with-modification: the superlative *"learning rate is the most important thing"* demoted to a scoped means-claim, per [truth-vs-effectiveness](./truth-vs-effectiveness.md) — truth is absolute over means, silent over ends — and placed at meadows-12-leverage-points rung 4, below goals and paradigms). | idle-resource-is-adversarial §Why the endogenous slope is not the master variable |

## 2026-09-01 The expense cycle `/validate-idea` — 1 confirmed + 1 candidate unlock at the money ↔ resource-stewardship layer

| Components | Pattern | Unlock | Failure mode | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| personal-resource-inventory §Register 6 (the obligations ledger — amounts and counterparties) × financial-prognosis-model (a runnable budget that amortizes every line) × meadows-12-leverage-points (the rung ladder) | Strategy selected by metric type — **confirmed** by measurement, not argument: `pages/expense-cycle-test.flow` carries 8 `expect` lines, all passing | **The phase of a lumpy expense is a leverage point neither the ledger nor the model can see alone.** The ledger records *how much* and *to whom*; the model smooths every line into a monthly average. Feed the ledger's cycle into the model as a pulse and a lever appears that costs nothing: holding the annual total fixed, moving one 4,500-GEL trip between months moves the cash floor from **1,717 to 6,217 GEL (3.6×)** while three-year net worth moves **≤1.2%**. So a recurring outflow is a *pulse*, not a flow — between gates the amount is not a variable — and it carries two levers rather than one: the **amount** (rung 12) and the **phase** (rung 10). The general rule the measurement supports: **amortize for totals, never for extremes** — the error lands entirely on min/max metrics and is approximately the cluster size. Third household instance of meadows-12-leverage-points §Example 4's own finding that rungs 10 and 6 punch above their height. | Amortizing a lumpy line and then reading a `min:` or `max:` metric off it (the source model's `bufferTarget` also inflates permanently under smoothing); registering a cycle for lines too small to matter — the same test on the $56/mo travel line moved the floor 2%; treating the phase lever as a reason to cut the amount instead of moving it | financial-prognosis-model §Amortizing a lumpy expense; personal-resource-inventory §Register 6 |
| personal-resource-inventory §Register 6 (a bleed row: amount + counterparty) × idle-resource-is-adversarial §Conversions (the money+experience → storable-artifact edge) × Kiyosaki asset/liability | Adapter across a class boundary (expense → artifact), routed by a 4-way Strategy — **candidate**, gated on the earn-back test producing one artifact that clears its incremental cost | **A liability row can be turned into an asset row — but only for expenses whose job leaves a trace.** The ledger knows *how much* a big expense costs; the conversions machinery knows how to spend an input into a different, storable class. Composed, an expense gets a **return disposition** (Deduct / Recover / Convert / Accept) and the *Convert* ones become Register-4 Built Artifacts that earn — Kiyosaki's "move it to the asset column" made mechanical. The load-bearing new knowledge is the **discriminator**: income is a storable-side phenomenon, so a purely perishable consumption cannot be earned on, and a **regeneration** expense (rest, family, health) cannot convert without destroying its job. Distinct from the timing unlock above — that one asks *when* you may act on the outflow, this asks *whether it can return*. **Two worked runs (2026-09-01) both failed Convert on the same missing funnel** — a September trip (→ Accept) and the Claude subscription (→ a `justified` Deduct-plus-slope tool) — which localizes the binding constraint to the funnel (first-million-roadmap), not the expenses; the gate held as a falsifier. | Forcing Convert onto a regeneration expense (turns recovery into work — a net loss even when it "makes money"); counting the whole trip as the conversion cost instead of the *incremental* production cost; "buying with a byproduct" dressed up as a conversion (fails the earn-back gate) | personal-resource-inventory §The return decision; idle-resource-is-adversarial §Spending a big expense into an artifact |

### 2026-09-02 Multi-valued attributes — 1 candidate at the completeness layer

| Components | Pattern | Unlock | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [representation-rules](./representation-rules.md) Rule 10 (count-shape) x [multi-valued-attributes](./multi-valued-attributes.md) §Route 3 (enumerable sets) | reading-property-reused-as-recall-check | **Rule 10's count-shape, invented as a *reading* aid, is also a *recall* completeness checksum — the only defence the wiki has against silent truncation.** Every existing check verifies what a recall produced; none verifies what it failed to produce, because a partial answer to "name the exceptions" is well-formed and feels complete from the inside. The count is the one part of a set a partial recall cannot fake, and Rule 10 already specifies how to hold it perceptually (the outline is the polygon of its own size, so an empty seat is visible before a label is read). Both of Rule 10's boundaries transfer unchanged: unordered sets only, and past seven members impose a key rather than draw a bigger polygon. Candidate because: the reuse is argued from the structure of the failure, not from a measured comparison. **Gate to promote** — matched enumerable sets, split: one arm encodes members plus an explicit count-shape, the other spends equal extra time on the members alone; score *completeness* separately from accuracy, cold, after one sleep. Refuted if truncation rates come out equal, which would mean the count is recovered from the members rather than held independently. | candidate |

### 2026-09-02 `MULTIDIMENSIONAL_vs_UNIDIMENSIONAL_ENCODING.md` ingest — 1 confirmed + 2 candidates at the encoding-quality layer

| Components | Pattern | Unlock | Failure mode | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [encoding-dimensionality](./encoding-dimensionality.md) §The decline gate × [multi-attribute-encoding](./multi-attribute-encoding.md) §Step 0 | budget-gate × decline-before-delete | **One two-stage encode budget, where the wiki previously had only the second stage.** Step 0 deletes *attributes* (class-constant, derivable, low-frequency); the decline gate declines *the whole encoding* (thin information, speed over depth, shallow use, no responsibility for predicting). Run in order — gate, then Step 0 — they answer the two questions that between them set every encoding's cost, and neither answers the other's. The gate is the wiki's **first named stopping rule for encoding richness**: every prior page argues for richer encoding and none said when to stop, so over-encoding had no name and left no symptom. Confirmed — both components exist in production and are now reciprocally wired. | Building a full room for an item nobody is responsible for (the mirror-image failure of holding a list for one you are); running Step 0 first and deleting attributes from an encoding that should never have been built | [encoding-dimensionality](./encoding-dimensionality.md) §The decline gate |

| Components | Pattern | Unlock | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [encoding-dimensionality](./encoding-dimensionality.md) §Three reasons (falsifiability) × the wiki's falsifier discipline ([table-memorization](./table-memorization.md) §Falsifier result · [multi-attribute-encoding](./multi-attribute-encoding.md) §Falsifiable claim) | falsifiability-as-a-property-of-the-encoding | **Dimensionality decides whether content can be wrong at all, so falsifiability becomes checkable at encode time rather than only at claim time.** A list absorbs its own counterevidence ("he has good days and bad days") and therefore cannot improve; a dimensional claim names trigger, appraisal and the alternative it excludes, so a failed test revises one wall. If this holds, a unidimensional encoding is not merely harder to recall — it is *structurally unimprovable*, which is a stronger reason to avoid it than any recall statistic. Candidate because: the claim is argued from one source and one worked case, and the wiki's own falsifier culture has never been examined for whether its testable pages are testable *because* they are dimensional. One deliberate counter-case — a genuinely falsifiable unidimensional encoding — would refute it. | candidate |
| [encoding-dimensionality](./encoding-dimensionality.md) §The pattern-library multiplier × [chunking](./chunking.md) × [lego-skills-patterns](./lego-skills-patterns.md) | amortization-across-items | **Per-item encode cost falls as a pattern library grows, so the Nth item costs a tag rather than an encoding.** Registered with a **measurement gate, and against a counterexample in its own source**: the document's worked arithmetic gives 150 minutes unidimensional against 190 multidimensional and concludes for multidimensional on an uncomputed "patterns compound", while its summary table asserts a 10x speedup that appears nowhere in the body. Gate to promote: record encode minutes per item against the count of patterns already held, and show the marginal cost of a *pattern-matched* item sitting below the unidimensional baseline across a run of consecutive items. Until that curve exists this stays candidate — the shape is right (it is what [chunking](./chunking.md) does elsewhere) and the evidence offered is self-refuting. | candidate |

## U — See (CAST)
1. Registry of all unlocks: pair / triple → new capability
2. Edges: framework × framework → unlock row

## D — Name (NEDF)
1. Composability Index = the unlock registry
2. Single answer to "what does X + Y unlock?"
3. Patterns promoted only after N independent instances

## F — Do (SPEAR)
1. New combination idea → check whether already registered
2. Validate against 3 instances → promote to pattern
3. Pattern lands → spin out its own owner page

## B — Watch (HEART)
1. Re-deriving the same unlock multiple times
2. Patterns claimed at 1 instance (too early)
3. Drift between candidate and confirmed unlocks

## L — Predict (ORACLE)
1. Promoted pattern → portable to new domains
2. Candidate pattern → may not survive 3rd instance

## R — Act (GRACE)
1. Combine X + Y → register here
2. 3rd instance lands → promote to pattern
